     1	#!/usr/bin/env python3
     2	"""DSpark MLA attention primitives for the numpy oracle.
     3	
     4	Faithful ports of model.py apply_rotary_emb + DSparkAttention.forward (window-
     5	only MLA, compress_ratio==0) and kernel.py sparse_attn (with the per-head
     6	attention-sink bias added to the softmax denominator only). See
     7	issue468/13_phase4_forward_design.md §4.
     8	
     9	All arrays F32, GGUF ne order (ne[0] innermost). Matmuls use the convention
    10	that a stored weight [out, in] (GGUF ne=[in,out]) is applied as x @ W.T.
    11	"""
    12	import numpy as np
    13	
    14	ROPE_DIM = 64          # qk_rope_head_dim (rope applied to last 64 of head_dim=512)
    15	HEAD_DIM = 512
    16	NORM_EPS = 1e-6
    17	
    18	
    19	def precompute_rope(rope_dim, seqlen, theta=10000.0):
    20	    """Standard RoPE freqs (no YaRN; original_seq_len=0 for compress_ratio==0).
    21	    Returns cos[seqlen, rope_dim/2], sin[seqlen, rope_dim/2]."""
    22	    half = rope_dim // 2
    23	    freqs = 1.0 / (theta ** (np.arange(0, rope_dim, 2, dtype=np.float64) / rope_dim))  # [half]
    24	    t = np.arange(seqlen, dtype=np.float64)
    25	    ang = np.outer(t, freqs)            # [seqlen, half]
    26	    return np.cos(ang).astype(np.float32), np.sin(ang).astype(np.float32)
    27	
    28	
    29	def apply_rotary(x_last, cos, sin):
    30	    """Rotate the last rope_dim of x. cos/sin must already be broadcast-shaped to
    31	    match x_last[..., 0::2] (caller builds the shape: e.g. [1,s,1,half] for q,
    32	    [1,s,half] for kv, or [half] to share across all positions). Inverse rotation
    33	    is obtained by passing -sin. Returns rotated [..., rope_dim]."""
    34	    x = x_last.astype(np.float32)
    35	    x_even = x[..., 0::2]
    36	    x_odd = x[..., 1::2]
    37	    cos_b = np.broadcast_to(cos, x_even.shape)
    38	    sin_b = np.broadcast_to(sin, x_odd.shape)
    39	    out_even = x_even * cos_b - x_odd * sin_b
    40	    out_odd = x_even * sin_b + x_odd * cos_b
    41	    out = np.empty_like(x)
    42	    out[..., 0::2] = out_even
    43	    out[..., 1::2] = out_odd
    44	    return out
    45	
    46	
    47	def sparse_attn(q, kv_gathered, attn_sink, scale):
    48	    """Sparse attention with a per-head sink. q [b,s,h,d], kv_gathered [b,s,topk,d],
    49	    attn_sink [h]. For each (b,s,h): scores = (q·kv)*scale [topk]; softmax denom
    50	    includes an extra exp(attn_sink[h]-m) term (sink absorbs mass, 0 to numerator).
    51	    Returns o [b,s,h,d]."""
    52	    b, s, h, d = q.shape
    53	    topk = kv_gathered.shape[2]
    54	    # scores [b,s,h,topk]
    55	    scores = np.einsum("bshd,bstd->bsht", q.astype(np.float32), kv_gathered.astype(np.float32)) * scale
    56	    m = scores.max(axis=-1)                       # [b,s,h]
    57	    sink_term = np.maximum(m, attn_sink[None, None, :])  # stable max incl sink
    58	    numer = np.exp(scores - sink_term[..., None])  # [b,s,h,topk]
    59	    denom = numer.sum(axis=-1) + np.exp(attn_sink[None, None, :] - sink_term)  # [b,s,h]
    60	    o = np.einsum("bsht,bstd->bshd", numer, kv_gathered.astype(np.float32)) / denom[..., None]
    61	    return o.astype(np.float32)
    62	
    63	
    64	def dspark_topk_idxs(window_size, block_size, start_pos):
    65	    """get_dspark_topk_idxs: [0..min(win,start+1)-1] ++ [win..win+block-1].
    66	    Each draft position attends to the window's real anchors + the draft block.
    67	    Returns [block_size, topk_k] (broadcast across batch; per-position identical)."""
    68	    n_win = min(window_size, start_pos + 1)
    69	    win_idx = np.arange(n_win, dtype=np.int32)
    70	    block_idx = (window_size + np.arange(block_size, dtype=np.int32))
    71	    return np.concatenate([win_idx, block_idx])  # [topk_k]; same for all draft positions
    72	
    73	
    74	def rmsnorm(x, weight, eps=NORM_EPS):
    75	    xf = x.astype(np.float32)
    76	    return xf * (1.0 / np.sqrt(np.mean(xf * xf, axis=-1, keepdims=True) + eps)) * weight
    77	
    78	
    79	def dspark_attention(x_draft, main_x, w, start_pos, cos_full, sin_full):
    80	    """DSparkAttention decode (start_pos>0). x_draft [1,block,dim], main_x [1,1,dim].
    81	    w: dict of layer attn weights (q_a,q_a_norm,q_b,kv,kv_a_norm,attn_sinks,
    82	    output_a,output_b). cos/sin_full: precomputed RoPE over enough positions.
    83	    Maintains a window KV cache (passed/returned). Returns (o [1,block,dim],
    84	    kv_cache updated).
    85	
    86	    Reuses the kernel semantics of model.py DSparkAttention.forward exactly,
    87	    EXCEPT skipping act_quant (FP8 activation sim) — see design doc §4.
    88	    """
    89	    block_size = x_draft.shape[1]
    90	    win = 128
    91	    n_heads = 64
    92	    head_dim = HEAD_DIM
    93	    n_groups = 8
    94	    o_lora = 1024
    95	    scale = head_dim ** -0.5
    96	
    97	    # 1. anchor KV from main_x (target hidden), cached at slot start_pos%win
    98	    main_kv = rmsnorm(main_x @ w["kv"].T, w["kv_a_norm"])           # [1,1,512]
    99	    fc = cos_full[start_pos], sin_full[start_pos]   # [32] each (1D)
   100	    main_kv[..., -ROPE_DIM:] = apply_rotary(main_kv[..., -ROPE_DIM:], fc[0], fc[1])
   101	
   102	    # build window: slot0=prefill anchor (start_pos=0 earlier), slot start_pos%win=this anchor
   103	    # For the oracle's single-decode scenario we materialize only the slots topk will read.
   104	    # (start_pos=1: real slots {0,1}; zeros elsewhere, masked out by topk.)
   105	    n_win_real = min(win, start_pos + 1)
   106	    win_kv = np.zeros((1, win, head_dim), dtype=np.float32)
   107	    # slot 0 is the prefill anchor; caller must have run prefill to set it.
   108	    # We pass it in via w["_win_slot0_kv"]. Slot start_pos%win = main_kv.
   109	    if "_win_slot0_kv" in w:
   110	        win_kv[:, 0:1] = w["_win_slot0_kv"]
   111	    win_kv[:, start_pos % win:start_pos % win + 1] = main_kv
   112	
   113	    # 2. draft-block q,kv
   114	    qr = rmsnorm(x_draft @ w["q_a"].T, w["q_a_norm"])              # [1,block,1024]
   115	    q = (qr @ w["q_b"].T).reshape(1, block_size, n_heads, head_dim)  # [1,block,64,512]
   116	    q = q * (1.0 / np.sqrt(np.mean(q * q, axis=-1, keepdims=True) + NORM_EPS))  # per-head RMSNorm
   117	    # per-position cos/sin for the block positions, shaped to broadcast over heads.
   118	    cs = cos_full[start_pos + 1:start_pos + 1 + block_size]   # [block, 32]
   119	    ss = sin_full[start_pos + 1:start_pos + 1 + block_size]   # [block, 32]
   120	    cs_q = cs[None, :, None, :]   # [1, block, 1, 32] for q [1,block,heads,rope]
   121	    ss_q = ss[None, :, None, :]
   122	    cs_kv = cs[None, :, :]        # [1, block, 32] for kv [1,block,rope]
   123	    ss_kv = ss[None, :, :]
   124	    q[..., -ROPE_DIM:] = apply_rotary(q[..., -ROPE_DIM:], cs_q, ss_q)
   125	    kv = rmsnorm(x_draft @ w["kv"].T, w["kv_a_norm"])              # [1,block,512]
   126	    kv[..., -ROPE_DIM:] = apply_rotary(kv[..., -ROPE_DIM:], cs_kv, ss_kv)
   127	    kv_all = np.concatenate([win_kv, kv], axis=1)                   # [1, win+block, 512]
   128	
   129	    # 3. sparse attention over the DSpark topk pattern (identical for all draft positions)
   130	    idx = dspark_topk_idxs(win, block_size, start_pos)             # [topk_k]
   131	    kv_gathered = kv_all[:, idx, :]                                # [1,block,topk,d] (broadcast block)
   132	    kv_gathered = np.broadcast_to(kv_gathered, (1, block_size, idx.size, head_dim))
   133	    o = sparse_attn(q, kv_gathered, w["attn_sinks"], scale)        # [1,block,64,512]
   134	    # inverse rotary on the rope dims of o (same per-position cos/sin; inverse = conj)
   135	    o[..., -ROPE_DIM:] = apply_rotary(o[..., -ROPE_DIM:], cs_q, -ss_q)
   136	
   137	    # 4. grouped low-rank output projection: wo_a [8,1024,..] einsum then wo_b
   138	    # o reshaped to [1,block,n_groups, head_dim//n_groups*..] — see model.py:
   139	    #   o = o.view(b,s,n_groups,-1); wo_a viewed [n_groups, o_lora, -1];
   140	    #   o = einsum("bsgd,grd->bsgr", o, wo_a); x = wo_b(o.flatten(2))
   141	    gd = head_dim * n_heads // n_groups   # 512*64/8 = 4096
   142	    o_g = o.reshape(1, block_size, n_groups, gd)                    # [1,block,8,4096]
   143	    # wo_a GGUF ne: [out_low_dim, head_dim*(heads/groups)] = [8192, 4096]; HF/torch [8,1024,512]
   144	    # model.py: wo_a.view(n_groups, o_lora, -1) -> [8, 1024, 512]; einsum bsgd,grd->bsgr
   145	    # but our stored output_a is the fused [out_low, in] = [8192, 4096]. Reshape to [8,1024,4096]? No.
   146	    # Re-derive: HF wo_a weight shape is [n_groups*o_lora, in_per_group]. in_per_group = head_dim*(n_heads/n_groups)
   147	    # = 512*8 = 4096. So wo_a is [8*1024, 4096] = [8192,4096]. view(8,1024,-1) -> [8,1024,4096]?? that's wrong dim.
   148	    # Correct: per group r, the slice is [o_lora, in_per_group]=[1024,4096]. n_groups slices -> [8,1024,4096]? No,
   149	    # total rows = n_groups*o_lora = 8192, cols = in_per_group = 4096. view(n_groups,o_lora,in_per_group) needs
   150	    # 8*1024*4096 elements = way more than 8192*4096. So the per-group input is SMALLER.
   151	    # Actual: in_per_group = head_dim * (n_heads/n_groups) = 512 * (64/8) = 512*8 = 4096. Hmm same.
   152	    # The math: total params = out_low * in = 8192 * 4096. view(8, o_lora=1024, head_dim=512) = 8*1024*512=4.19M,
   153	    # but we have 8192*4096 = 33.5M. So view is (n_groups, o_lora, head_dim) = (8,1024,512)?? That's 4.19M, mismatch.
   154	    # The reference uses einsum "bsgd,grd->bsgr" with o [b,s,8,gd] and wo_a [g=8, r=1024, d=head_dim=512].
   155	    # So gd = head_dim = 512, NOT 4096! o is reshaped to [b,s,8,512]?? But o is [b,s,64,512] (n_heads=64).
   156	    # o.view(b,s,n_groups,-1): 64*512/8 = 4096 per group -> [b,s,8,4096]. Then einsum bsgd,grd needs d match:
   157	    # o[b,s,8,4096] @ wo_a[8,1024,512]? d mismatch (4096 vs 512). So gd in einsum is NOT head_dim.
   158	    # Resolving: wo_a.view(n_groups, o_lora, -1) on a [8192, 4096] tensor -> [8, 1024, 4096]? elements 8*1024*4096=33.5M != 33.5M YES.
   159	    # So wo_a viewed [8,1024,4096], einsum bsgd(4096),grd(4096)->bsgr(1024). d=4096. OK matches!
   160	    wo_a = w["output_a"].reshape(n_groups, o_lora, gd)              # [8,1024,4096] (already HF order)
   161	    # einsum bsgd,grd->bsgr: o_g[b,s,g,d] wo_a[g,r,d] -> [b,s,g,r]
   162	    o_lor = np.einsum("bsgd,grd->bsgr", o_g, wo_a)                # [1,block,8,1024]
   163	    o_flat = o_lor.reshape(1, block_size, n_groups * o_lora)       # [1,block,8192]
   164	    out = o_flat @ w["output_b"].T                                   # [1,block,4096] (output_b HF [out=4096,in=8192])
   165	    return out.astype(np.float32), main_kv[:, 0]   # return slot-1 KV for caller (prefill of next)
   166	
   167	
   168	def dspark_attention_prefill(main_x, w, cos_full, sin_full):
   169	    """DSparkAttention prefill (start_pos==0): cache the anchor KV at slot 0, no
   170	    attention output (returns x unchanged per model.py). Returns the cached
   171	    main_kv [1,512] for slot 0."""
   172	    main_kv = rmsnorm(main_x @ w["kv"].T, w["kv_a_norm"])          # [1,1,512]
   173	    c = cos_full[0], sin_full[0]   # [32] each (1D -> broadcasts in apply_rotary)
   174	    main_kv[..., -ROPE_DIM:] = apply_rotary(main_kv[..., -ROPE_DIM:], c[0], c[1])
   175	    return main_kv[:, 0]   # [512] slot-0 KV
   176	
   177	
   178	if __name__ == "__main__":
   179	    # Self-test 1: RoPE norm preservation (rotating preserves L2 norm).
   180	    rng = np.random.default_rng(1)
   181	    cos, sin = precompute_rope(64, 16)
   182	    x = rng.standard_normal((2, 5, 64)).astype(np.float32)
   183	    xr = apply_rotary(x, cos[3], sin[3])
   184	    n_in = np.linalg.norm(x, axis=-1)
   185	    n_out = np.linalg.norm(xr, axis=-1)
   186	    print(f"RoPE norm preservation: max abs diff = {np.abs(n_in-n_out).max():.2e}")
   187	    assert np.allclose(n_in, n_out, atol=1e-4), "RoPE broke norm"
   188	
   189	    # Self-test 2: sparse_attn sink — with a large positive sink, output -> 0

   191	    q = rng.standard_normal((1, 1, 4, 8)).astype(np.float32)
   192	    kvg = rng.standard_normal((1, 1, 3, 8)).astype(np.float32)
   193	    sink_big = np.array([1e9, 1e9, 1e9, 1e9], dtype=np.float32)
   194	    o_sink = sparse_attn(q, kvg, sink_big, 0.1)
   195	    print(f"sparse_attn big-sink output norm (want ~0): {np.linalg.norm(o_sink):.2e}")
   196	    assert np.linalg.norm(o_sink) < 1e-3, "big sink should zero output"
   197	    sink_small = np.array([-1e9, -1e9, -1e9, -1e9], dtype=np.float32)
   198	    o_normal = sparse_attn(q, kvg, sink_small, 0.1)
   199	    # compare to plain softmax-weighted sum
   200	    scores = (q[0,0] @ kvg[0,0].T) * 0.1
   201	    sm = np.exp(scores - scores.max(-1, keepdims=True)); sm /= sm.sum(-1, keepdims=True)
   202	    o_ref = sm @ kvg[0,0]
   203	    print(f"sparse_attn no-sink vs plain softmax: max abs diff = {np.abs(o_normal[0,0]-o_ref).max():.2e}")
   204	    assert np.allclose(o_normal[0, 0], o_ref, atol=1e-4), "no-sink mismatch"
   205	
   206	    # Self-test 3: dspark_topk_idxs at start_pos=1
   207	    idx = dspark_topk_idxs(128, 5, 1)
   208	    print(f"dspark_topk_idxs(start_pos=1): {idx.tolist()} (want [0,1,128,129,130,131,132])")
   209	    assert idx.tolist() == [0, 1, 128, 129, 130, 131, 132]
   210	    print("\nattention primitives: RoPE norm + sink + topk invariants OK")

/bin/zsh -lc "nl -ba issue468/dspark_oracle/moe.py | sed -n '1,140p'" in /Users/lobanov/Projects/ds4-dspark-research
 succeeded in 0ms:
     1	#!/usr/bin/env python3
     2	"""DSpark MoE for the numpy oracle.
     3	
     4	Faithful port of model.py Gate + MoE + Expert (SwiGLU), F32. Drafter layers
     5	(43+, n_hash_layers=3 targets layers 0-2) use SCORE-BASED routing (no tid2eid).
     6	See issue468/13_phase4_forward_design.md §5.
     7	
     8	score_func = "sqrtsoftplus": scores = softplus(x@gate).sqrt(); bias shifts topk
     9	selection only (not weights); weights normalized to sum 1 then *route_scale=1.5.
    10	n_activated_experts=6, n_routed=256, n_shared=1, moe_inter_dim=2048, dim=4096.
    11	"""
    12	import numpy as np
    13	
    14	NORM_EPS = 1e-6
    15	ROUTE_SCALE = 1.5
    16	TOPK = 6
    17	SWIGLU_LIMIT = 10.0   # config swiglu_limit; reference Expert.forward clamps gate(max)/up(both)
    18	
    19	
    20	def softplus(x):
    21	    # numerically stable
    22	    return np.where(x > 20, x, np.log1p(np.exp(np.minimum(x, 20))))
    23	
    24	
    25	def gate(x, gate_inp, exp_probs_b, topk=TOPK):
    26	    """x [...,dim] -> (weights [...,topk], indices [...,topk]). sqrtsoftplus,
    27	    bias shifts topk but not weights; weights gathered from ORIGINAL scores,
    28	    normalized, *route_scale. (model.py Gate.forward)."""
    29	    scores = softplus((x @ gate_inp.T).astype(np.float32))     # [...,256] (gate_inp HF [out=256,in=dim])
    30	    scores = np.sqrt(scores)
    31	    sel_scores = scores + exp_probs_b                           # bias for topk only
    32	    # topk indices along last axis
    33	    idx = np.argpartition(sel_scores, -topk, axis=-1)[..., -topk:]  # unsorted topk
    34	    # sort the topk by score descending for determinism
    35	    order = np.argsort(-np.take_along_axis(sel_scores, idx, axis=-1), axis=-1)
    36	    idx = np.take_along_axis(idx, order, axis=-1)               # [...,topk] sorted desc
    37	    weights = np.take_along_axis(scores, idx, axis=-1)          # original (no bias)
    38	    weights = weights / (weights.sum(axis=-1, keepdims=True) + 1e-12)
    39	    weights = weights * ROUTE_SCALE
    40	    return weights.astype(np.float32), idx
    41	
    42	
    43	def swiglu_expert(x, w_gate, w_up, w_down):
    44	    """Single expert SwiGLU: down(silu(gate(x)) * up(x)). x [...,dim] -> [...,dim].
    45	    w_gate/w_up/w_down are expert slices in HF [out,in] order (standard linear).
    46	    Apply as x @ W.T. Applies the config swiglu_limit clamp (gate max-only, up both)
    47	    per reference Expert.forward + ds4's swiglu() kernel."""
    48	    g = x @ w_gate.T
    49	    u = x @ w_up.T
    50	    # swiglu clamp (config swiglu_limit=10.0; matches ds4 swiglu() + model.py Expert.forward)
    51	    if SWIGLU_LIMIT > 0:
    52	        u = np.clip(u, -SWIGLU_LIMIT, SWIGLU_LIMIT)
    53	        g = np.clip(g, None, SWIGLU_LIMIT)   # gate: max only
    54	    # silu(g) = g * sigmoid(g)
    55	    h = g * (1.0 / (1.0 + np.exp(-g)))
    56	    inter = h * u
    57	    return inter @ w_down.T
    58	
    59	
    60	def moe(x, input_ids, w, expert_store, topk=TOPK):
    61	    """x [1,block,dim], input_ids (unused for score routing). w has the dense
    62	    tensors (gate, shared, norms). expert_store yields per-expert [inter,dim]
    63	    gate/up/down lazily: expert_store.expert(e) -> (wg,wu,wd).
    64	    Returns [1,block,dim]."""
    65	    b, s, dim = x.shape
    66	    xf = x.reshape(b * s, dim).astype(np.float32)
    67	    weights, idx = gate(xf, w["ffn_gate_inp"], w["ffn_exp_probs_b"], topk)  # [bs, topk]
    68	    y = np.zeros_like(xf)
    69	    cache = {}  # per-forward memo: (e) -> (wg,wu,wd)
    70	    for t in range(xf.shape[0]):
    71	        for k in range(topk):
    72	            e = int(idx[t, k])
    73	            if e not in cache:
    74	                cache[e] = expert_store.expert(e)
    75	            wg, wu, wd = cache[e]
    76	            y[t] += weights[t, k] * swiglu_expert(xf[t], wg, wu, wd)
    77	    y += swiglu_expert(xf, w["ffn_gate_shexp"], w["ffn_up_shexp"], w["ffn_down_shexp"])
    78	    return y.reshape(b, s, dim).astype(np.float32)
    79	
    80	
    81	if __name__ == "__main__":
    82	    rng = np.random.default_rng(2)
    83	    # gate: weights sum to route_scale, indices unique (gate_inp stored [in=dim,out=256])
    84	    x = rng.standard_normal((2, 4096)).astype(np.float32)
    85	    gi = rng.standard_normal((256, 4096)).astype(np.float32) * 0.01
    86	    eb = rng.standard_normal(256).astype(np.float32) * 0.01
    87	    w, idx = gate(x, gi, eb)
    88	    print(f"gate: weights shape {w.shape}, sum={w.sum(-1)} (want ~{ROUTE_SCALE})")
    89	    print(f"  indices unique per row: {[len(np.unique(idx[i])) for i in range(2)]} (want 6)")
    90	    assert np.allclose(w.sum(-1), ROUTE_SCALE, atol=1e-3)
    91	    assert all(len(np.unique(idx[i])) == 6 for i in range(2))
    92	    # swiglu + moe shape + determinism (weights in GGUF-ne [in,out] order)
    93	    dim, inter = 4096, 2048
    94	    wg = rng.standard_normal((inter, dim)).astype(np.float32) * 0.01
    95	    wu = rng.standard_normal((inter, dim)).astype(np.float32) * 0.01
    96	    wd = rng.standard_normal((dim, inter)).astype(np.float32) * 0.01
    97	    xe = rng.standard_normal(dim).astype(np.float32)
    98	    y1 = swiglu_expert(xe, wg, wu, wd)
    99	    y2 = swiglu_expert(xe, wg, wu, wd)
   100	    print(f"swiglu: deterministic {np.array_equal(y1, y2)}, output shape {y1.shape}")
   101	    assert np.array_equal(y1, y2) and y1.shape == (dim,)
   102	    print("moe primitives: gate invariants + swiglu OK")

## Verdict Per Claim

- **C1 FIDELITY: questionable.** The 0 mismatch claim is true from CSV vs retained summaries, but it is not independent: `run_stage0` reuses the same model/drafter loaders and forward path as `measure_acceptance_bundle`.
- **C2 MARKOV EXACT: sound internally.** `base_logits + markov_w1[prev] @ markov_w2.T` matches `forward_head`’s own logic at [forward.py](/Users/lobanov/Projects/ds4-dspark-research/issue468/dspark_oracle/forward.py:101); artifact completion implies the 400-position assert passed.
- **C3 ANCHORS: sound arithmetic, weak validation.** Recomputed: p1 = 65/80 = 0.8125; k=1 prefix hist `{0:15,1:13,2:18,3:11,4:10,5:13}` gives E=2.3375. But both derive from the same retained acceptance corpus.
- **C4 RANK COMPUTATION: mostly sound.** `count(score > score[target])` is correct 0-index strict rank; all target ids are in-vocab, no match/rank contradictions. But full scores are not persisted, so one miss cannot be independently recomputed from artifacts.
- **C5 ALIGNMENT: sound.** Verified all `target_topk.steps[s].selected.id == target_selected_tokens[s]`, top1 matches selected for temp=0, and row target equals `selected[step+p]`. This matches [measure_acceptance_bundle.py](/Users/lobanov/Projects/ds4-dspark-research/issue468/dspark_oracle/measure_acceptance_bundle.py:199) and [run_stage0_quant_mismatch.py](/Users/lobanov/Projects/ds4-dspark-research/issue468/run_stage0_quant_mismatch.py:169).
- **C6 INTERPRETATION: questionable / overclaimed.** Shallow p1 ranks are real, but “therefore quant-flip-like and fine-tuning-recoverable” is not established. Only 4/15 p1 misses have Q2 gap <1 nat; median Q2 gap is 2.1506, which cuts against a pure near-tie quant-flip story.
- **C7 COUNTERFACTUAL: questionable semantics, sound arithmetic.** k=1 exactly equals current retained prefix distribution; k=2 E=2.875 is verified. But “greedy-spine top-k” is not a true acceptance ceiling for fine-tune or tree deployment.

## Premise Sensitivity

- **P1:** Under FP-distillation premise, shallow Q2 misses motivate testing mismatch. If false, the quant-mismatch narrative collapses; the data only says “Q2 target often appears near the drafter top.”
- **P2:** Under IQ2XXS + temp=0 premise, this is a valid argmax diagnostic. If deployment includes sampling/real post-rejection trajectories, headline acceptance weakens: retained E[a|4] drops from 2.175 at temp=0 to 1.925/1.9125 at temp 0.5/1.0.
- **P3:** Under speedup-model validity, the anchors align. If it fails, the rank-shape diagnostic survives, but “clears toward speedup” does not.

## New Experiments To Try

1. **FP-vs-Q2 topk on the exact p1 misses.** Run FP teacher logits for the same `step+1` positions; check whether drafter pick matches FP top1 while Q2 picks target. Expected signal: directly separates quant flip from drafter error. Effort: medium.
2. **Independent production drafter checksum.** Export top-10/full-rank checks from the actual ds4/Metal drafter for a few retained positions. Expected: kills the shared-bug risk. Effort: medium/high.
3. **Persist full decision scores or top-64 scores.** Add artifact for `base`, `bias`, `full_score topN`, and exact target score. Expected: lets reviewers recompute ranks without loading the model. Effort: low.
4. **Stage 1 tap-precision acceptance.** Run the planned precision variant and compare p1, E[a|4], and p1 miss rank conversion. Expected: tests localization, not full quant-causality. Effort: medium.
5. **Larger/temp-varied corpus.** Repeat on >100 prompts and temp 0/0.5/1.0. Expected: narrows CI and exposes prompt/domain instability. Effort: medium/high.

## Leading Hypothesis

This looks like a **mixture**: some genuine shallow/near-tie mismatch, plus ordinary or systematic drafter error. The median rank=1 is real, but the large median Q2 gap says many misses are not obvious quant flips.

Decisive test: **FP teacher topk on the exact p1 miss set**. If FP top1 equals the drafter pick and Q2 top1 equals the retained target, quant mismatch is confirmed. If FP and Q2 both prefer the retained target, the drafter is just wrong.

## Bottom Line

“Proceed to Stage 1” survives as a cheap next experiment.  
“Stage 0 clears because the error is overwhelmingly perturbative / fine-tuning-recoverable” does **not** survive as stated. Downgrade it to: p1 misses are shallow on this small temp=0 corpus, but causality and recoverability remain unproven.
